L /**
T 3 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java punctuation.definition.comment.java
L  * This is the main class for using Gson. Gson is typically used by first constructing a {@code
T 95 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * Gson} instance and then invoking {@link #toJson(Object)} or {@link #fromJson(String, Class)}
T 36 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 37 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 42 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java keyword.other.documentation.javadoc.java
T 44 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 58 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java variable.parameter.java
T 59 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 63 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 64 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 69 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java keyword.other.documentation.javadoc.java
T 71 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 94 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java variable.parameter.java
T 95 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * methods on it. {@code Gson} instances are thread-safe so you can reuse them freely across
T 92 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * multiple threads.
T 20 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>You can create a {@code Gson} instance by invoking {@link #Gson() new Gson()} if the default
T 57 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 58 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 63 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java keyword.other.documentation.javadoc.java
T 65 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 71 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java variable.parameter.java
T 83 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 98 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * configuration is all you need. You can also use {@link GsonBuilder} to build a {@code Gson}
T 94 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * instance with various configuration options such as versioning support, pretty printing, and
T 95 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * custom serialization and deserialization logic.
T 50 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>Gson is based on the concept of {@link TypeAdapter}s which contain the actual logic for
T 93 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * serializing and deserializing a Java type. For more advanced use cases {@link TypeAdapterFactory}
T 100 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * can be used which allows inspecting the Java type at runtime and providing a {@code TypeAdapter}
T 99 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * for it. {@code TypeAdapterFactory} also supports delegating to adapters for other types, which is
T 100 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * useful when handling types which are themselves composed of multiple types or when extending an
T 98 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * existing adapter.<br>
T 24 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * The interfaces {@link JsonSerializer} and {@link JsonDeserializer} allow processing JSON data as
T 99 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * tree structures; this can make their usage easier than {@code TypeAdapter}, but they are not as
T 98 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * efficient.
T 13 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>Gson provides built-in type adapters for many standard Java types such as {@code List}, {@code
T 100 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * Integer} or {@code String}. For unknown classes it uses reflection to inspect the fields of the
T 98 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * class and uses them for the JSON structure.
T 46 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>When serializing or deserializing a type, {@code Gson} instances cache the {@code TypeAdapter}
T 100 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * they obtained from a {@code TypeAdapterFactory} for that type. It can therefore improve
T 90 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * performance to reuse a single {@code Gson} instance (for example by storing it in a {@code static
T 100 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * final} field), especially when relying on the built-in reflection-based adapter or when using
T 96 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * custom type adapter factories.
T 33 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>Here is an example of how Gson is used for a simple Class:
T 64 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <pre>
T 8 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * Gson gson = new Gson(); // Or use new GsonBuilder().create();
T 64 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * MyType target = new MyType();
T 32 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * String json = gson.toJson(target); // serializes target to JSON
T 66 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * MyType target2 = gson.fromJson(json, MyType.class); // deserializes json into target2
T 88 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * </pre>
T 9 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>If the type of the object that you are converting is a {@code ParameterizedType} (i.e. has at
T 99 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * least one type argument, for example {@code List<MyType>}) then for deserialization you must use
T 99 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * a {@code fromJson} method with {@link Type} or {@link TypeToken} parameter to specify the
T 92 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * parameterized type. For serialization specifying a {@code Type} or {@code TypeToken} is optional,
T 100 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * otherwise Gson will use the runtime type of the object. {@link TypeToken} is a class provided by
T 99 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * Gson which helps creating parameterized types. Here is an example showing how this can be done:
T 98 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <pre>
T 8 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * TypeToken&lt;List&lt;MyType&gt;&gt; listType = new TypeToken&lt;List&lt;MyType&gt;&gt;() {};
T 95 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * List&lt;MyType&gt; target = new LinkedList&lt;MyType&gt;();
T 62 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * target.add(new MyType(1, "abc"));
T 36 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * Gson gson = new Gson();
T 26 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * // For serialization you normally do not have to specify the type, Gson will use
T 83 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * // the runtime type of the objects, however you can also specify it explicitly
T 81 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * String json = gson.toJson(target, listType.getType());
T 57 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * // But for deserialization you have to specify the type
T 58 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * List&lt;MyType&gt; target2 = gson.fromJson(json, listType);
T 62 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * </pre>
T 9 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>See the <a href="https://github.com/google/gson/blob/main/UserGuide.md">Gson User Guide</a>
T 97 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * for a more complete set of examples.
T 39 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <h2 id="default-lenient">JSON Strictness handling</h2>
T 57 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * For legacy reasons most of the {@code Gson} methods allow JSON data which does not comply with
T 97 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * the JSON specification when no explicit {@linkplain Strictness strictness} is set (the default).
T 99 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * To specify the strictness of a {@code Gson} instance, you should set it through {@link
T 89 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java

T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
